And now with the church involved, it feels like they’re trying to push me out completely, like all I’ve done for the family should just be forgotten.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these people you mentioned are coordinating their actions in a planned or organized way, or do you think they’re just acting on their own but in a way that ends up working against you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s planned. I mean, maybe not everyone sits in one room and says it out loud like that, but it’s too organized to be just accidents. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore—they were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that church respect, to push me out.\n\nAnd you know, in families people always remember who brought what dish or who helped with the children, but suddenly with me it’s like none of it happened. All the things I did for this family, they want everyone to forget. That doesn’t happen by itself. Someone is making it happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a specific example of something your in-laws or the parish committee did that made you feel like they were trying to erase you or make others forget what you've done for the family?\n\nPatient: One example is at the church, with the altar and the parish committee. They act like it’s just church business, you know, flowers here, names there, who gets thanked, who stands where—but it’s not just that. I could see how they were using it to push me out.\n\nAnd my in-laws, they weren’t only arguing with me at home or saying rude things. They got those church people involved, or let them be involved, so everyone would stop remembering what I’ve done for this family. All the years I helped, showed up, did things no one else wanted to do—suddenly I’m not mentioned, like I never mattered.\n\nIt’s like when you clean the whole house and then someone else lights one candle and everyone praises them. That happened to my cousin once at a wedding, actually, with the seating and all that—but anyway, with me, it was the altar, the parish committee, and my in-laws behind it. That’s when I knew it was more than normal family conflict.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a specific action taken by your in-laws or the parish committee that you felt was directly aimed at making others forget your contributions?\n\nPatient: The specific thing was the way they used the altar and the parish committee, like I said. It wasn’t just, “Oh, we forgot to mention her,” no. My in-laws were behind it, or at least pushing it along, and then suddenly the church people are arranging things so I’m left out, not thanked, not seen.\n\nI came in because I knew it wasn’t just arguing anymore. Families argue, everybody argues—my mother used to say if nobody argues at a holiday then someone is hiding something—but this was different. They were using the altar, the parish committee, that whole respectable church setting, to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. That’s what hurt me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are actively working together to damage your reputation or exclude you from the family and church community, rather than just overlooking or forgetting your contributions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I don’t think it’s just forgetting. People forget little things, like who brought the salad to a picnic—my sister used to forget that every single year—but this is bigger than that.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all that church respectability, to push me out. And when you do it through the church, people believe it. They think, “Oh, it must be proper, it must be decided,” and then suddenly I’m not seen, not remembered.\n\nSo yes, I think they’re working together, or at least moving in the same direction, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee have the ability to control how people remember or forget things about you, or influence others’ thoughts about you through the church?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t know if they can control thoughts like that exactly, but through the church, people listen. They see the altar, they hear what the parish committee says, who gets named, who gets left out, and then they start remembering things the way those people want them to.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. And once church people act like you don’t matter, others follow along. It’s not like a regular family disagreement over dinner, you know—though my brother-in-law once made a whole scene over who carved the turkey—but this is different. This feels planned.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that specific people in the parish committee have communicated with your in-laws to exclude you, or do you think they’re acting independently but their actions still seem coordinated to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can name who called who, if that’s what you mean. I’m not sitting in their kitchens listening to phone calls—though my mother-in-law always did love a phone call, she could stretch one conversation from supper to bedtime.\n\nBut it feels coordinated. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar, and the parish committee, to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. Whether they sat in a room and planned it or just all know what they’re doing without saying it straight out… the result is the same. I’m left out, and they look respectable while doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee are part of a larger, organized effort to harm your reputation, or do you think their actions are more about personal disagreements that have escalated?\n\nPatient: I think it started as family things, maybe, but it’s gone past that. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, families can be petty—my aunt once stopped speaking to someone over a missing casserole dish for years—but this isn’t that. This has the church wrapped around it, so it looks official, like I’m the one who doesn’t belong. Whether it’s some big organized thing or just them all lining up together, it feels like an effort to erase me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you believe the actions of your in-laws and the parish committee are being done with specific intent to harm you, or if you think it might be more of a coincidence that they are all acting this way toward you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s coincidence. Maybe one little thing could be coincidence, like someone forgetting to put your name on a list—my cousin did that once for a wedding and there was trouble for months—but not this, not all of it together.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. That feels intentional to me. Maybe they wouldn’t say it out loud that way, but I know what it feels like when people are trying to make you disappear.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe others in the church or family have noticed or acknowledged this effort to push you out, or do you feel like you’re the only one who sees it this way?\n\nPatient: Some people notice, I think. They don’t always say it straight out, because in church everybody wants to smile and pass the bulletin like nothing’s wrong. But you can tell. A look, a pause, someone suddenly busy when I come near.\n\nI came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just having another family argument with me. They were using the altar and that parish committee to push me out, to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. And that kind of thing… well, people see more than they admit.\n\nBut then again, maybe I’m the only one willing to say it. My sister used to say, “Don’t make waves,” even when the roof was leaking right over the dining table. People will let a whole ceiling fall in before they’ll admit there’s a problem. So yes, I think others notice, but they keep quiet.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this effort to push you out and make people forget your contributions is happening in other areas of your life, outside of your family and the church?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about everywhere else… I mean, mostly it’s there, with my in-laws and the church. That’s where I can see it plain as day. I came in because it wasn’t just arguing anymore—they were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that, to push me out and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nOutside of that… well, people talk, don’t they? Like at the grocery store, you see someone from parish and suddenly they’re looking at the tomatoes like they never saw you before. But maybe that still comes back to the church. It all kind of spreads from there, like when you spill coffee on a tablecloth and it runs into all the little stitching.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people outside of your family and church, like friends or coworkers, are also involved in trying to erase you or make others forget what you’ve done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say coworkers, because I’m not really around that kind of thing now. Friends… well, friends hear things, don’t they? One person says something after Mass, and then it goes around like when my aunt used to pass recipes but leave out one ingredient on purpose—then everyone wondered why the cake fell.\n\nBut the center of it is still my in-laws and the church. That’s why I came in, because I knew it wasn’t just family arguing anymore. They were using the altar, the parish committee, all of that, to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family. Other people may get pulled into it, but it starts there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your in-laws and the parish committee have a deliberate, organized plan to harm your reputation, or do you think their actions, while harmful, are not part of a coordinated plan?\n\nPatient: I think it’s organized, yes. Maybe they wouldn’t call it a “plan,” because people like that never write it down on paper like a shopping list. But I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me anymore. They were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nAnd you know, it’s funny, my mother used to say if two people always show up with the same story, somebody rehearsed it first. Like at Thanksgiving when everybody suddenly knew not to ask about the gravy boat I bought—anyway, that’s not the point. The point is, it feels deliberate. Too many things line up for it to be an accident.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the actions of your in-laws and the parish committee are part of a broader, coordinated effort that goes beyond normal social disagreements, such as a deliberate campaign to isolate you?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s what I’m saying. It’s not just normal people not getting along, you know? Families argue, committees have their little opinions—my sister used to say church committees could fight over the color of a napkin if you let them.\n\nBut this is more than that. I came in because I knew my in-laws weren’t just arguing with me. They were using the altar, the parish committee, the way people listen when something is said in church… all of it, to push me out and make people forget what I’ve done for this family. It feels like they’re trying to isolate me, yes, even if they smile and call it “concern” or “keeping peace.”\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
